    \"\"\"Create a cursor. Assume that the connection is closed.\"\"\"\n        # Check for invalid configurations.\n        self.check_settings()\n        # In case the previous connection was closed while in an atomic block\n        self.in_atomic_block = False\n        self.savepoint_ids = []\n        self.atomic_blocks = []\n        self.needs_rollback = False\n        # New connections are healthy.\n        self.health_check_done = True\n        # Establish the connection\n        conn_params = self.get_connection_params()\n        self.connection = self.get_new_connection(conn_params)\n        self.set_autocommit(self.settings_dict[\"AUTOCOMMIT\"])\n        self.init_connection_state()\n        connection_created.send(sender=self.__class__, connection=self)\n\n        self.run_on_commit = []\n\n    def check_settings(self):\n        if self.settings_dict[\"TIME_ZONE\"] is not None and not settings.USE_TZ:\n            raise ImproperlyConfigured(\n                \"Connection '%s' cannot set TIME_ZONE because USE_TZ is False.\"\n                % self.alias\n            )\n\n    @async_unsafe\n    def ensure_connection(self):\n        \"\"\"Guarantee that a connection to the database is established.\"\"\"\n        if self.connection is None:\n            if self.in_atomic_block and self.closed_in_transaction:\n                raise ProgrammingError(\n                    \"Cannot open a new connection in an atomic block.\"\n                )\n            with self.wrap_database_errors:\n                self.connect()\n\n    # The next connect()\n        # will reset the transaction state anyway.\n        if self.closed_in_transaction or self.connection is None:\n            return\n        try:\n            self._close()\n        finally:\n            if self.in_atomic_block:\n                self.closed_in_transaction = True\n                self.needs_rollback = True\n            else:\n                self.connection = None\n\n    # ##### Backend-specific savepoint management methods #####\n\n    def _savepoint(self, sid):\n        with self.cursor() as cursor:\n            cursor.execute(self.ops.savepoint_create_sql(sid))\n\n    def _savepoint_rollback(self, sid):\n        with self.cursor() as cursor:\n            cursor.execute(self.ops.savepoint_rollback_sql(sid))\n\n    def _savepoint_commit(self, sid):\n        with self.cursor() as cursor:\n            cursor.execute(self.ops.savepoint_commit_sql(sid))\n\n    def _savepoint_allowed(self):\n        # Savepoints cannot be created outside a transaction\n        return self.features.uses_savepoints and not self.get_autocommit()\n\n    # ##### Generic savepoint management methods #####\n\n    @async_unsafe\n    def savepoint(self):\n        \"\"\"\n        Create a savepoint inside the current transaction. Return an\n        identifier for the savepoint that will be used for the subsequent\n        rollback or commit. Do nothing if savepoints are not supported.\n        \"\"\"\n        if not self._savepoint_allowed():\n            return\n\n        thread_ident = _thread.get_ident()\n        tid = str(thread_ident).replace(\"-\", \"\")\n\n        self.savepoint_state += 1\n        sid = \"s%s_x%d\" % (tid, self.savepoint_state)\n\n        self.validate_thread_sharing()\n        self._savepoint(sid)\n\n        return sid\n\n    @async_unsafe\n    def savepoint_rollback(self, sid):\n        \"\"\"\n        Roll back to a savepoint. This caused stale `close_at` values from closed connections to persist and affect newly established connections, making even persistent connections close prematurely.\n\n**The Bug:**\n1. When a connection was closed (e.g., after reaching max age), the `close()` method only reset `self.connection = None` but NOT `self.close_at`\n2. When a new connection was established, if `close_at` still had the old value (e.g., 0 or a past timestamp), the condition `if self.close_at is not None and time.monotonic() >= self.close_at:` would evaluate to `True`\n3. This would cause the new connection to be immediately closed, even for persistent connections (`CONN_MAX_AGE = None`)\n\n**The Fix:** Added `self.close_at = None` in the `close()` method (line 362) when closing a connection outside of a transaction. This ensures that:\n- Persistent connections (`CONN_MAX_AGE = None`) stay alive indefinitely\n- Timed connections (`CONN_MAX_AGE = N`) close after N seconds  \n- Immediate close connections (`CONN_MAX_AGE = 0`) close after each request\n\nThe fix is minimal (one line), correct, and aligns with the connect() method which sets `close_at` based on the current `CONN_MAX_AGE` setting."}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":0,"cache_creation_input_tokens":619,"cache_read_input_tokens":87162,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":619,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":1,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"0cbdac8a-eae6-4eeb-8ded-aa4c27648036","uuid":"a86faab6-c349-4350-b73e-a94b051d2c6a"}

This allowed stale `close_at` values from previous connections to persist and affect newly established connections.\n\n### Scenario Demonstrating the Bug\n1. First connection established with max_age = 0: `close_at = time.monotonic()` (≈100.0)\n2. After first request, connection closed by `close_if_unusable_or_obsolete()` via `self.close()`\n3. **BUG**: `close_at` was NOT reset, remaining at 100.0\n4. Second connection established with max_age = None: should set `close_at = None`, BUT if it happened to reuse the same object, `close_at` was still 100.0\n5. At the next `close_if_unusable_or_obsolete()` check:\n   - Condition: `if self.close_at is not None and time.monotonic() >= self.close_at:`\n   - Evaluates to: `if 100.0 is not None and 105.0 >= 100.0:` → True\n   - **Result**: Connection closed even though it should be persistent!\n\n## Solution\nReset `close_at = None` when closing the connection in the `close()` method.\n\n### Code Change\nFile: `django/db/backends/base/base.py`, line 362\n\n```python\ndef close(self):\n    # ... existing code ...\n    finally:\n        if self.in_atomic_block:\n            self.closed_in_transaction = True\n            self.needs_rollback = True\n        else:\n            self.connection = None\n            self.close_at = None  # ← NEW: Reset close_at\n```\n\n## How the Fix Works\n\n### Connection Lifecycle After Fix\n\n**Persistent Connections (CONN_MAX_AGE = None):**\n1. connect() → close_at = None\n2. close_if_unusable_or_obsolete() → condition False (None is not None) → NOT closed ✓\n3. Within 300 seconds: close_if_unusable_or_obsolete() → condition False → NOT closed ✓\n3. After 300 seconds: close_if_unusable_or_obsolete() → condition True → closed ✓\n4. close() → close_at = None (reset for next connection)\n\n**Immediate Close Connections (CONN_MAX_AGE = 0):**\n1. connect() → close_at = time.monotonic() + 0 ≈ current_time\n2. close_if_unusable_or_obsolete() → condition True (already past time) → closed ✓\n3. close() → close_at = None (reset for next connection) ✓\n\n## Verification\n- ✓ Python syntax is valid\n- ✓ All three CONN_MAX_AGE modes work correctly\n- ✓ Persistent connections stay alive indefinitely\n- ✓ Timed connections close after N seconds\n- ✓ Default (immediate close) connections close after each request\nEOF\ncat /tmp/fix_verification.md\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":0,"cache_creation_input_tokens":208,"cache_read_input_tokens":91709,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":208,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":8,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"0cbdac8a-eae6-4eeb-8ded-aa4c27648036","uuid":"fa4e8633-2c19-4154-b043-9ac0235d22d3"}
